People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, yes, thank you so much for fitting me in. I really appreciate you taking the time, and I hope I'm not being a bother. 

Um... I'm a little nervous, to be honest. I usually try to handle things on my own, and I really don't want to sound dramatic or complain too much. But... well, it's a little embarrassing to admit out loud. I guess what actually scared me enough to come in is that I just keep thinking it would be easier if I didn't wake up. I know that sounds terrible, and I'm so sorry to even say it. 

And sometimes, when I'm driving home from the clinic, I just... I catch myself wondering what would happen if I didn't turn the wheel. I know I shouldn't think like that, and I promise I'm trying my best to push it away. I just figured I should be completely honest with you, right? Am I explaining that okay?

Um... gosh, I'm really sorry, I'm actually not entirely sure. I hope it's okay that I don't have a clear answer for you. I... I guess I haven't really noticed if it's happened exactly like this before? 

I usually just try to keep my head down at the clinic and push through whatever's going on, so I might not have paid close enough attention to it in the past. Um... I really want to give you a good answer, but I'm honestly just... I'm not sure. I hope that's okay? I don't want to be unhelpful. 

I mean, I've definitely had times where I felt a little blue, but I always just told myself I was being overly sensitive or letting the stress at the clinic get to me. I try so hard to just keep things positive and not dwell on the bad stuff, so I guess I kind of blur all the hard times together. I don't really remember if there were specific... episodes, like you said, or if it was just me not handling things as well as I should have been. 

I'm really sorry I can't give you a clear yes or no. I just don't want to exaggerate and make it sound like I've been a mess my whole life when maybe I just need to try a little harder to stay upbeat. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not wasting your time by being so unsure.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I... oh goodness, I feel terrible that we're still stuck on this. I know you're just being thorough, and I so appreciate how careful you're being with my care. I really do. 

I just... I'm drawing a complete blank on the timeline part. I don't think I ever paid close enough attention to notice if it was exactly two weeks or if there were a couple of months in between. I always just told myself to snap out of it and get back to work at the clinic—the animals need me, you know? I never wanted to be the kind of person who keeps a calendar of when they feel sad. 

I feel like I'm just giving you the same unhelpful answer over and over, and I am so, so sorry. I know you need this information to help me. Is there... is there maybe another way I can answer that would be more useful for you? I really want to be a good patient for you today.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh goodness... I know you're asking about the separate times again, and I'm so, so sorry I keep failing to give you a good answer for the past. Please don't be upset with me, I just can't separate those older years out clearly in my head. 

But... maybe this will help? What I do know for sure, if it's okay to share, is about right now. This current feeling... it's been hanging over me for about 11 months straight. I really tried to just keep a smile on my face at the clinic and not let anyone see, but it's been there for almost a year. Even before this past month... which, well, this past month has just made everything feel pretty unbearable, if I'm being completely honest. I tried so hard to manage it on my own so I wouldn't be a bother to anyone, but it's just been a very long 11 months.

Does that help at all? I know it's not exactly what you asked about the separate times, but I hope it gives you at least a little of what you need for your notes. Am I doing okay?
